People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for taking the time to see me. I, um... well, I hope I'm in the right place, and I really don't want to be a bother. 

I just... I've been having this feeling lately, and I know it probably sounds a little silly to say out loud. I hope you don't think badly of me for it. But even on my better days, I just can't seem to shake this idea that some of the folks at my church, and in my walking club, are... well, that they're watching me. And it sort of feels like they're putting little messages about me in the Sunday bulletin, or hiding things about me in what they say when we're together. 

I'm sure I'm just overthinking things, but it's been bothering me. Does... does that make sense? I just wanted to make sure I was doing the right thing by coming in to ask about it.

It sounds so clear that I actually answer back out loud, right before I remember that nobody else is there with me.

sick).\n  manifestation: I keep going over every little thing I might have done wrong, especially not calling my friend more, and I feel like I must have failed her and everybody else.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don't have a plan to hurt myself, but I catch myself thinking more and more that it might be all right if I went to sleep and didn't wake up.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... I'm sure it's just me being a little chatty, really. You know how it is when you get to talking and one thing just leads to another? I hope I'm not wasting your time with all this.

But, um... lately, when I try to explain things to my friends, I'll start off talking about something perfectly normal, like the mail or something that happened at church. And then, before I know it, I'm going on about quilt patterns, or the birds at the feeder, and... and then those little warnings I feel like I'm getting. 

I can tell by the look on their faces that they just have no idea what I'm getting at. It's so embarrassing, honestly. I try to be a good conversationalist, I really do. I just... my mind wanders a bit, I suppose. That's fairly normal for someone my age, isn't it? Just losing your train of thought every now and then?

Well, yes, I suppose I do. Like I was just saying, I really don't mean to be confusing. I'll start off trying to tell a nice story about the mail arriving, or maybe something the pastor said at church... and then I just sort of drift.

Oh, dear, I feel like I'm just talking in circles and repeating myself to you today. I'm so sorry if I'm not doing a very good job of explaining things. 

But yes, I suppose "disconnected" is exactly what it is. Just like I was mentioning a moment ago... I'll start out with a perfectly normal thought about church or the mail, and then suddenly I'm going on about my quilting, or the birds, and those warnings. In my head, as I'm speaking, it feels like it all flows together just beautifully. But then I see that blank look on people's faces, and I realize they have absolutely no earthly idea how I got from point A to point B. 

I really do try my best to keep my stories straight so I don't confuse anyone or make a fool of myself. Am I making sense to you now, doctor? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right answers.

Just losing your train of thought every now and then?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stay on topic when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose I do. Like I was just saying, I really don't mean to be confusing. I'll start off trying to tell a nice story about the mail arriving, or maybe something the pastor said at church... and then I just sort of drift. \n\nI'll end up talking about the quilt patterns I've been looking at, or the blue jays at the feeder, and... well, and those little warnings I mentioned. And I can just see it in their eyes, my friends, they just look so puzzled. They don't know what I'm getting at at all. \n\nI try so hard to be polite and keep things pleasant, I really do. It's just a little bit of absentmindedness, don't you think? I'm sure you talk to plenty of ladies my age who get a little sidetracked like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected from each other when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear, I feel like I'm just talking in circles and repeating myself to you today. I'm so sorry if I'm not doing a very good job of explaining things. \n\nBut yes, I suppose \"disconnected\" is exactly what it is. Just like I was mentioning a moment ago... I'll start out with a perfectly normal thought about church or the mail, and then suddenly I'm going on about my quilting, or the birds, and those warnings. In my head, as I'm speaking, it feels like it all flows together just beautifully. But then I see that blank look on people's faces, and I realize they have absolutely no earthly idea how I got from point A to point B. \n\nI really do try my best to keep my stories straight so I don't confuse anyone or make a fool of myself. Am I making sense to you now, doctor? Am I making sense to you now, doctor? days food just doesn't appeal to me, so I pick at toast or soup and my skirts have been hanging looser than they used to.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not work.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep going over every little thing I might have done wrong, especially not calling my friend more, and I feel like I must have failed her and everybody else.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don't have a plan to hurt myself, but I catch myself thinking more and more that it might be all right if I went to sleep and didn't wake up.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I was at my lowest, barely eating and thinking I had failed everyone, I was also hearing my friend's voice and believing church people were voices or suspicious thoughts were quieter.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: There was a spell of several weeks when I was getting dressed, eating a bit better, and not feeling so weighed down, but I still heard my name in the house and thought people at church were hinting about me.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I still live at home, but I have disconnected from each other when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear, I feel like I'm just talking in circles and repeating myself to you today. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... I know how terribly silly it must sound when you say it out loud like that. I really don't want you to think I'm being vain, assuming everything is about me. 

But... yes, I suppose I do believe they're for me. Honestly, that's the main reason I finally agreed to come in and talk to you today. Because even on my better days, when I'm feeling quite alright and keeping busy, I just can't seem to shake the feeling. 

It just seems like some of the folks at church, and even a few of the ladies from the walking club, are... well, watching me. And those little things printed in the Sunday bulletin, or the very specific words they use when we're chatting... it truly feels like they are little messages put there just for me to notice. 

I try my hardest to just brush it off and keep a smile on my face, because I'd absolutely hate to cause a fuss or accuse anyone of doing anything improper. I hope that's the right way to handle it. You don't think I sound completely foolish for worrying about it, do you, doctor?

Oh, dear... that's the hard part, isn't it? I would love nothing more than to just agree with you and say it's all in my head. I really don't want to be one of those difficult people who imagines silly things and makes a fuss over nothing.

Well, I... I suppose it's possible. I know it sounds so presumptuous of me to think everyone is focused on me. I really don't want to be one of those people who makes everything about themselves.

And I can just see it in their eyes, my friends, they just look so puzzled. They don't know what I'm getting at at all. \n\nI try so hard to be polite and keep things pleasant, I really do. It's just a little bit of absentmindedness, don't you think? I'm sure you talk to plenty of ladies my age who get a little sidetracked like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected from each other when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear, I feel like I'm just talking in circles and repeating myself to you today. I'm so sorry if I'm not doing a very good job of explaining things. \n\nBut yes, I suppose \"disconnected\" is exactly what it is. Just like I was mentioning a moment ago... I'll start out with a perfectly normal thought about church or the mail, and then suddenly I'm going on about my quilting, or the birds, and those warnings. In my head, as I'm speaking, it feels like it all flows together just beautifully. But then I see that blank look on people's faces, and I realize they have absolutely no earthly idea how I got from point A to point B. \n\nI really do try my best to keep my stories straight so I don't confuse anyone or make a fool of myself. Am I making sense to you now, doctor? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are racing or speeding by when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say they're racing. No, not at all. It's not like they're going too fast or spinning out of control or anything scary like that. \n\nIt's really just... well, like I was saying, they just sort of take a little detour sometimes. I hope I haven't made it sound worse than it actually is! I just get a bit scatterbrained and lose my place, but my mind certainly isn't speeding. \n\nDoes that help clarify things a bit, doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information and not making a mountain out of a molehill.\n\nDoctor: Have these experiences of being watched, hearing your name, or having trouble keeping your thoughts straight caused you any problems at work, at home, or in your relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say they've caused *problems*, exactly. I hope that's the right way to handle it. You don't think I sound completely foolish for worrying about it, do you, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people you believe are watching you or sending you messages are actually doing so in a way that's real and not just in your mind?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... that's the hard part, isn't it? I hope you don't think less of me for admitting that. Am I making any sense at all, doctor? I suppose it's possible. I know it sounds so presumptuous of me to think everyone is focused on me. I really don't want to be one of those people who makes everything about themselves. \n\nBut... oh, it's just so hard to explain without sounding completely foolish. Like I mentioned, the whole reason I'm here taking up your time is because I just can't seem to make the feeling go away. And those little things in the bulletin, or the specific words they choose when we talk... it just feels so strongly like they're putting them there specifically for me to notice. \n\nI try my best to tell myself it's just a silly misunderstanding, but the feeling just won't leave me be. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, dear... well... I suppose I might do that just a little bit. I really hope I'm not being too confusing right now! I always want to be polite and make sure I'm making sense when I speak to people. 

But sometimes... well, when I try to explain things, I might start out with one thought—maybe about the mail, or something that happened at church—and then, before I know it, I end up talking about quilt patterns, or the birds outside, or... or those little warnings I feel like I'm getting. And then I look at the person I'm talking to, and I can just see on their face that they don't have the faintest idea what I'm getting at. 

I feel so terrible when that happens, because I really don't mean to be difficult to follow. I just have so many thoughts and they sort of tumble out all at once. I hope I'm not doing that to you right now, doctor. Am I being clear enough for you?

I suppose it's possible. I know it sounds so presumptuous of me to think everyone is focused on me. Like I mentioned, the whole reason I'm here taking up your time is because I just can't seem to make the feeling go away. Even when my mood is perfectly fine and I'm having a lovely day, I still get that deep-down feeling that the folks at church and the ladies in the walking club are watching me. And those little things in the bulletin, or the specific words they choose when we talk... it just feels so strongly like they're putting them there specifically for me to notice. \n\nI try my best to tell myself it's just a silly misunderstanding, but the feeling just won't leave me be. Does that make me sound terribly stubborn, doctor? I really am trying to look at it logically, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts or conversations often seem to jump abruptly from one topic to another without a clear connection?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well... I suppose I might do that just a little bit. I really hope I'm not being too confusing right now! I always want to be polite and make sure I'm making sense when I speak to people. \n\nBut sometimes... well, when I try to explain things, I might start out with one thought—maybe about the mail, or something that happened at church—and then, before I know it, I end up talking about quilt patterns, or the birds outside, or... or those little warnings I feel like I'm getting. And then I look at the person I'm talking to, and I can just see on their face that they don't have the faintest idea what I'm getting at. \n\nI feel so terrible when that happens, because I really don't mean to be difficult to follow. I start out trying to explain something simple, like the mail or church, and then... well, my mind just wanders a bit. Before I know it, I'm talking about quilt patterns, or the birds at the feeder, or... or those little warnings I was telling you about. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, my... I feel like I'm just repeating myself to you, and I really don't want to be a nuisance or waste your time! But yes, organizing them... it's exactly like I was trying to say a moment ago. 

Whether I'm speaking or even trying to write a little note, I'll start off with a perfectly normal thought about the mail or something at church. But then my mind just sort of... flutters, I suppose. And suddenly I'm going on about my quilt patterns, or the birds, or those warnings. I look up, and I can just tell the person has no idea what I'm getting at. 

I try so hard to keep it all neat and tidy in my head, I really do. Does that answer what you were asking, doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for taking the time to see me. I, um... well, I hope I'm in the right place, and I really don't want to be a bother. \n\nI just... I've been having this feeling lately, and I know it probably sounds a little silly to say out loud. I hope you don't think badly of me for it. But even on my better days, I just can't seem to shake this idea that some of the folks at my church, and in my walking club, are... well, that they're watching me. And it sort of feels like they're putting little messages about me in the Sunday bulletin, or hiding things about me in what they say when we're together. \n\nI'm sure I'm just overthinking things, but it's been bothering me. Does... does that make sense? I just wanted to make sure I was doing the right thing by coming in to ask about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people can't hear?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well. I know how a question like that sounds, and I really don't want you to think I'm... well, you know. I'm sure it's just my ears playing tricks on me, or maybe just a part of getting older. \n\nBut, um... sometimes, when the house is perfectly quiet, I do think I hear my name. Or just a few little words. It usually sounds exactly like one of my friends, coming from the kitchen or out on the porch. It sounds so clear that I actually answer back out loud, right before I remember that nobody else is there with me. \n\nIt's just a silly mistake, really. That happens to a lot of people, right? When they're just tired or the house is too quiet? I hope that doesn't sound too strange...\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these feelings of being watched and hearing your name?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I really haven't wanted to make a fuss about it, so I tried to just ignore it for a while. But if I really think back... I suppose it's been about eleven months now? Give or take a little. \n\nThat's when I first started noticing the... the messages, and thinking I heard those voices. And, um, I've also had a little trouble keeping my thoughts straight since around then, too. I just thought I was getting a bit scatterbrained, you know, just a normal part of getting older. I certainly didn't want to complain over nothing. \n\nEleven months feels like a long time to let something like this go on before asking a doctor... I hope I didn't wait too long to come in. Did I do the right thing by waiting to see if it would just pass on its own?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by having trouble keeping your thoughts straight?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I'm sure it's just me being a little chatty, really. You know how it is when you get to talking and one thing just leads to another? I hope I'm not wasting your time with all this.\n\nBut, um... lately, when I try to explain things to my friends, I'll start off talking about something perfectly normal, like the mail or something that happened at church. And then, before I know it, I'm going on about quilt patterns, or the birds at the feeder, and... and then those little warnings I feel like I'm getting. \n\nI can tell by the look on their faces that they just have no idea what I'm getting at. It's so embarrassing, honestly. I try to be a good conversationalist, I really do. I just... my mind wanders a bit, I suppose. That's fairly normal for someone my age, isn't it? Just losing your train of thought every now and then?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stay on topic when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose I do. Like I was just saying, I really don't mean to be confusing. I'll start off trying to tell a nice story about the mail arriving, or maybe something the pastor said at church... and then I just sort of drift. \n\nI'll end up talking about the quilt patterns I've been looking at, or the blue jays at the feeder, and... well, and those little warnings I mentioned. And I can just see it in their eyes, my friends, they just look so puzzled. They don't know what I'm getting at at all. \n\nI try so hard to be polite and keep things pleasant, I really do. It's just a little bit of absentmindedness, don't you think? I'm sure you talk to plenty of ladies my age who get a little sidetracked like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected from each other when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear, I feel like I'm just talking in circles and repeating myself to you today. I'm so sorry if I'm not doing a very good job of explaining things. \n\nBut yes, I suppose \"disconnected\" is exactly what it is. Just like I was mentioning a moment ago... I'll start out with a perfectly normal thought about church or the mail, and then suddenly I'm going on about my quilting, or the birds, and those warnings. In my head, as I'm speaking, it feels like it all flows together just beautifully. But then I see that blank look on people's faces, and I realize they have absolutely no earthly idea how I got from point A to point B. \n\nI really do try my best to keep my stories straight so I don't confuse anyone or make a fool of myself. Am I making sense to you now, doctor? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are racing or speeding by when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say they're racing. No, not at all. It's not like they're going too fast or spinning out of control or anything scary like that. \n\nIt's really just... well, like I was saying, they just sort of take a little detour sometimes. I hope I haven't made it sound worse than it actually is! I just get a bit scatterbrained and lose my place, but my mind certainly isn't speeding. \n\nDoes that help clarify things a bit, doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information and not making a mountain out of a molehill.\n\nDoctor: Have these experiences of being watched, hearing your name, or having trouble keeping your thoughts straight caused you any problems at work, at home, or in your relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say they've caused *problems*, exactly. That sounds so... serious. I still live at home and manage my own affairs, of course. It's just that I've... well, I've taken a little step back from a few things lately. \n\nI haven't been going to my quilting gatherings as much, or the walking club. And I suppose I've let the gardening go just a tiny bit. But you know how it is at my age, doctor, sometimes you just need a little more rest. It's nothing to worry about, I promise. \n\nMy daughter, bless her heart, she's been coming around a bit more often to check on my meals and make sure I'm keeping my appointments. She's just being a good daughter, checking in on her mother. It's not because I can't handle things, it's just nice to have the company. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a burden to her, does it? I always try to be as independent as I can be.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the messages you think you're seeing in the Sunday bulletin or hearing in conversations are actually about you and intended for you to see or hear?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I know how terribly silly it must sound when you say it out loud like that. I really don't want you to think I'm being vain, assuming everything is about me. \n\nBut... yes, I suppose I do believe they're for me. Honestly, that's the main reason I finally agreed to come in and talk to you today. Because even on my better days, when I'm feeling quite alright and keeping busy, I just can't seem to shake the feeling. \n\nIt just seems like some of the folks at church, and even a few of the ladies from the walking club, are... well, watching me. And those little things printed in the Sunday bulletin, or the very specific words they use when we're chatting... it truly feels like they are little messages put there just for me to notice. \n\nI try my hardest to just brush it off and keep a smile on my face, because I'd absolutely hate to cause a fuss or accuse anyone of doing anything improper. I hope that's the right way to handle it. You don't think I sound completely foolish for worrying about it, do you, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people you believe are watching you or sending you messages are actually doing so in a way that's real and not just in your mind?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... that's the hard part, isn't it? I would love nothing more than to just agree with you and say it's all in my head. I really don't want to be one of those difficult people who imagines silly things and makes a fuss over nothing. \n\nBut... well, like I was just saying a moment ago, that's really the whole reason I finally came in to see you. Because even on my very best days, when I'm feeling perfectly fine, I just can't shake the feeling that it *is* real. It truly feels like those folks at church and the ladies from the walking club are actually watching me in real life. And the things they say, or those little notes in the bulletin... it feels like they are genuinely putting them there just for me. \n\nI try so hard to tell myself it's just my imagination playing tricks on an old lady, but it just feels so very real to me. I hope you don't think less of me for admitting that. Am I making any sense at all, doctor? I just want to be as honest with you as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the messages you believe you're seeing and hearing are not actually intended for you?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose it's possible. I know it sounds so presumptuous of me to think everyone is focused on me. I really don't want to be one of those people who makes everything about themselves. \n\nBut... oh, it's just so hard to explain without sounding completely foolish. Like I mentioned, the whole reason I'm here taking up your time is because I just can't seem to make the feeling go away. Honestly, that's the main reason I finally agreed to come in and talk to you today. Because even on my better days, when I'm feeling quite alright and keeping busy, I just can't seem to shake the feeling. \n\nIt just seems like some of the folks at church, and even a few of the ladies from the walking club, are... well, watching me. And those little things printed in the Sunday bulletin, or the very specific words they use when we're chatting... it truly feels like they are little messages put there just for me to notice. \n\nI try my hardest to just brush it off and keep a smile on my face, because I'd absolutely hate to cause a fuss or accuse anyone of doing anything improper. I hope that's the right way to handle it. You don't think I sound completely foolish for worrying about it, do you, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people you believe are watching you or sending you messages are actually doing so in a way that's real and not just in your mind?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... that's the hard part, isn't it? I would love nothing more than to just agree with you and say it's all in my head. I really don't want to be one of those difficult people who imagines silly things and makes a fuss over nothing. \n\nBut... well, like I was just saying a moment ago, that's really the whole reason I finally came in to see you. Because even on my very best days, when I'm feeling perfectly fine, I just can't shake the feeling that it *is* real. It truly feels like those folks at church and the ladies from the walking club are actually watching me in real life. And the things they say, or those little notes in the bulletin... it feels like they are genuinely putting them there just for me. \n\nI try so hard to tell myself it's just my imagination playing tricks on an old lady, but it just feels so very real to me. I hope you don't think less of me for admitting that. Am I making any sense at all, doctor? I just want to be as honest with you as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the messages you believe you're seeing and hearing are not actually intended for you?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose it's possible. I know it sounds so presumptuous of me to think everyone is focused on me. I really don't want to be one of those people who makes everything about themselves. \n\nBut... oh, it's just so hard to explain without sounding completely foolish. Like I mentioned, the whole reason I'm here taking up your time is because I just can't seem to make the feeling go away. Even when my mood is perfectly fine and I'm having a lovely day, I still get that deep-down feeling that the folks at church and the ladies in the walking club are watching me. And those little things in the bulletin, or the specific words they choose when we talk... it just feels so strongly like they're putting them there specifically for me to notice. \n\nI try my best to tell myself it's just a silly misunderstanding, but the feeling just won't leave me be. Does that make me sound terribly stubborn, doctor? I really am trying to look at it logically, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts or conversations often seem to jump abruptly from one topic to another without a clear connection?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well... I suppose I might do that just a little bit. I really hope I'm not being too confusing right now! I always want to be polite and make sure I'm making sense when I speak to people. \n\nBut sometimes... well, when I try to explain things, I might start out with one thought—maybe about the mail, or something that happened at church—and then, before I know it, I end up talking about quilt patterns, or the birds outside, or... or those little warnings I feel like I'm getting. Am I making sense to you now, doctor? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are racing or speeding by when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say they're racing. No, not at all. It's not like they're going too fast or spinning out of control or anything scary like that. \n\nIt's really just... well, like I was saying, they just sort of take a little detour sometimes. I hope I haven't made it sound worse than it actually is! I just get a bit scatterbrained and lose my place, but my mind certainly isn't speeding. \n\nDoes that help clarify things a bit, doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information and not making a mountain out of a molehill.\n\nDoctor: Have these experiences of being watched, hearing your name, or having trouble keeping your thoughts straight caused you any problems at work, at home, or in your relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say they've caused *problems*, exactly. It's just that I've... well, I've taken a little step back from a few things lately. \n\nI haven't been going to my quilting gatherings as much, or the walking club. I know how terribly silly it must sound when you say it out loud like that. And those little things printed in the Sunday bulletin, or the very specific words they use when we're chatting... it truly feels like they are little messages put there just for me to notice. \n\nI try my hardest to just brush it off and keep a smile on my face, because I'd absolutely hate to cause a fuss or accuse anyone of doing anything improper. I hope that's the right way to handle it. You don't think I sound completely foolish for worrying about it, do you, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people you believe are watching you or sending you messages are actually doing so in a way that's real and not just in your mind?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... that's the hard part, isn't it? I would love nothing more than to just agree with you and say it's all in my head. I really don't want to be one of those difficult people who imagines silly things and makes a fuss over nothing. \n\nBut... well, like I was just saying a moment ago, that's really the whole reason I finally came in to see you. It truly feels like those folks at church and the ladies from the walking club are actually watching me in real life. I hope you don't think less of me for admitting that. Am I making any sense at all, doctor? I just want to be as honest with you as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the messages you believe you're seeing and hearing are not actually intended for you?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose it's possible. I know it sounds so presumptuous of me to think everyone is focused on me. I really don't want to be one of those people who makes everything about themselves. \n\nBut... oh, it's just so hard to explain without sounding completely foolish. Like I mentioned, the whole reason I'm here taking up your time is because I just can't seem to make the feeling go away. And those little things in the bulletin, or the specific words they choose when we talk... it just feels so strongly like they're putting them there specifically for me to notice. \n\nI try my best to tell myself it's just a silly misunderstanding, but the feeling just won't leave me be. It's just a little bit of absentmindedness, don't you think? I'm sure you talk to plenty of ladies my age who get a little sidetracked like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected from each other when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear, I feel like I'm just talking in circles and repeating myself to you today. I'm so sorry if I'm not doing a very good job of explaining things. \n\nBut yes, I suppose \"disconnected\" is exactly what it is. Just like I was mentioning a moment ago... I'll start out with a perfectly normal thought about church or the mail, and then suddenly I'm going on about my quilting, or the birds, and those warnings. In my head, as I'm speaking, it feels like it all flows together just beautifully. But then I see that blank look on people's faces, and I realize they have absolutely no earthly idea how I got from point A to point B. \n\nI really do try my best to keep my stories straight so I don't confuse anyone or make a fool of myself. Am I making sense to you now, doctor? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are racing or speeding by when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say they're racing. No, not at all. It's not like they're going too fast or spinning out of control or anything scary like that. \n\nIt's really just... well, like I was saying, they just sort of take a little detour sometimes. I hope I haven't made it sound worse than it actually is! I just get a bit scatterbrained and lose my place, but my mind certainly isn't speeding. \n\nDoes that help clarify things a bit, doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information and not making a mountain out of a molehill.\n\nDoctor: Have these experiences of being watched, hearing your name, or having trouble keeping your thoughts straight caused you any problems at work, at home, or in your relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say they've caused *problems*, exactly. That sounds so... serious. I still live at home and manage my own affairs, of course. It's just that I've... well, I've taken a little step back from a few things lately. \n\nI haven't been going to my quilting gatherings as much, or the walking club. And I suppose I've let the gardening go just a tiny bit. But you know how it is at my age, doctor, sometimes you just need a little more rest. It's nothing to worry about, I promise. \n\nMy daughter, bless her heart, she's been coming around a bit more often to check on my meals and make sure I'm keeping my appointments. She's just being a good daughter, checking in on her mother. It's not because I can't handle things, it's just nice to have the company. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a burden to her, does it? I always try to be as independent as I can be.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the messages you think you're seeing in the Sunday bulletin or hearing in conversations are actually about you and intended for you to see or hear?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I know how terribly silly it must sound when you say it out loud like that. I really don't want you to think I'm being vain, assuming everything is about me. \n\nBut... yes, I suppose I do believe they're for me. Honestly, that's the main reason I finally agreed to come in and talk to you today. Because even on my better days, when I'm feeling quite alright and keeping busy, I just can't seem to shake the feeling. \n\nIt just seems like some of the folks at church, and even a few of the ladies from the walking club, are... well, watching me. And those little things printed in the Sunday bulletin, or the very specific words they use when we're chatting... it truly feels like they are little messages put there just for me to notice. \n\nI try my hardest to just brush it off and keep a smile on my face, because I'd absolutely hate to cause a fuss or accuse anyone of doing anything improper. I hope that's the right way to handle it. You don't think I sound completely foolish for worrying about it, do you, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people you believe are watching you or sending you messages are actually doing so in a way that's real and not just in your mind?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... that's the hard part, isn't it? I would love nothing more than to just agree with you and say it's all in my head. I really don't want to be one of those difficult people who imagines silly things and makes a fuss over nothing. \n\nBut... well, like I was just saying a moment ago, that's really the whole reason I finally came in to see you. Oh, my goodness... I'm so sorry, doctor, I feel like I'm just making you listen to the exact same things over and over again! I really don't want to be a bother.

I hope you don't think badly of me for it. But even on my better days, I just can't seem to shake this idea that some of the folks at my church, and in my walking club, are... well, that they're watching me. And it sort of feels like they're putting little messages about me in the Sunday bulletin, or hiding things about me in what they say when we're together. \n\nI'm sure I'm just overthinking things, but it's been bothering me. Does... does that make sense? I just wanted to make sure I was doing the right thing by coming in to ask about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people can't hear?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well. I know how a question like that sounds, and I really don't want you to think I'm... well, you know. I'm sure it's just my ears playing tricks on me, or maybe just a part of getting older. \n\nBut, um... sometimes, when the house is perfectly quiet, I do think I hear my name. Or just a few little words. It usually sounds exactly like one of my friends, coming from the kitchen or out on the porch. It sounds so clear that I actually answer back out loud, right before I remember that nobody else is there with me. \n\nIt's just a silly mistake, really. That happens to a lot of people, right? When they're just tired or the house is too quiet? I hope that doesn't sound too strange...\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these feelings of being watched and hearing your name?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I really haven't wanted to make a fuss about it, so I tried to just ignore it for a while. But if I really think back... I suppose it's been about eleven months now? Give or take a little. \n\nThat's when I first started noticing the... the messages, and thinking I heard those voices. And, um, I've also had a little trouble keeping my thoughts straight since around then, too. I just thought I was getting a bit scatterbrained, you know, just a normal part of getting older. I certainly didn't want to complain over nothing. \n\nEleven months feels like a long time to let something like this go on before asking a doctor... I hope I didn't wait too long to come in. Did I do the right thing by waiting to see if it would just pass on its own?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by having trouble keeping your thoughts straight?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I'm sure it's just me being a little chatty, really. You know how it is when you get to talking and one thing just leads to another? I hope I'm not wasting your time with all this.\n\nBut, um... lately, when I try to explain things to my friends, I'll start off talking about something perfectly normal, like the mail or something that happened at church. And then, before I know it, I'm going on about quilt patterns, or the birds at the feeder, and... and then those little warnings I feel like I'm getting. \n\nI can tell by the look on their faces that they just have no idea what I'm getting at. It's so embarrassing, honestly. I try to be a good conversationalist, I really do. I just... my mind wanders a bit, I suppose. That's fairly normal for someone my age, isn't it? Just losing your train of thought every now and then?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stay on topic when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose I do. Like I was just saying, I really don't mean to be confusing. I'll start off trying to tell a nice story about the mail arriving, or maybe something the pastor said at church... and then I just sort of drift. \n\nI'll end up talking about the quilt patterns I've been looking at, or the blue jays at the feeder, and... well, and those little warnings I mentioned. And I can just see it in their eyes, my friends, they just look so puzzled. They don't know what I'm getting at at all. \n\nI try so hard to be polite and keep things pleasant, I really do. It's just a little bit of absentmindedness, don't you think? I'm sure you talk to plenty of ladies my age who get a little sidetracked like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected from each other when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear, I feel like I'm just talking in circles and repeating myself to you today. I'm so sorry if I'm not doing a very good job of explaining things. \n\nBut yes, I suppose \"disconnected\" is exactly what it is. Just like I was mentioning a moment ago... I'll start out with a perfectly normal thought about church or the mail, and then suddenly I'm going on about my quilting, or the birds, and those warnings. In my head, as I'm speaking, it feels like it all flows together just beautifully. But then I see that blank look on people's faces, and I realize they have absolutely no earthly idea how I got from point A to point B. \n\nI really do try my best to keep my stories straight so I don't confuse anyone or make a fool of myself. Am I making sense to you now, doctor? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are racing or speeding by when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say they're racing. No, not at all. It's not like they're going too fast or spinning out of control or anything scary like that. \n\nIt's really just... well, like I was saying, they just sort of take a little detour sometimes. I hope I haven't made it sound worse than it actually is! I just get a bit scatterbrained and lose my place, but my mind certainly isn't speeding. \n\nDoes that help clarify things a bit, doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information and not making a mountain out of a molehill.\n\nDoctor: Have these experiences of being watched, hearing your name, or having trouble keeping your thoughts straight caused you any problems at work, at home, or in your relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say they've caused *problems*, exactly. That sounds so... serious. I still live at home and manage my own affairs, of course. It's just that I've... well, I've taken a little step back from a few things lately. \n\nI haven't been going to my quilting gatherings as much, or the walking club. And I suppose I've let the gardening go just a tiny bit. But you know how it is at my age, doctor, sometimes you just need a little more rest. It's nothing to worry about, I promise. \n\nMy daughter, bless her heart, she's been coming around a bit more often to check on my meals and make sure I'm keeping my appointments. She's just being a good daughter, checking in on her mother. It's not because I can't handle things, it's just nice to have the company. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a burden to her, does it? I always try to be as independent as I can be.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the messages you think you're seeing in the Sunday bulletin or hearing in conversations are actually about you and intended for you to see or hear?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I know how terribly silly it must sound when you say it out loud like that. I really don't want you to think I'm being vain, assuming everything is about me. \n\nBut... yes, I suppose I do believe they're for me. Honestly, that's the main reason I finally agreed to come in and talk to you today. Because even on my better days, when I'm feeling quite alright and keeping busy, I just can't seem to shake the feeling. \n\nIt just seems like some of the folks at church, and even a few of the ladies from the walking club, are... well, watching me. And those little things printed in the Sunday bulletin, or the very specific words they use when we're chatting... it truly feels like they are little messages put there just for me to notice. \n\nI try my hardest to just brush it off and keep a smile on my face, because I'd absolutely hate to cause a fuss or accuse anyone of doing anything improper. I hope that's the right way to handle it. You don't think I sound completely foolish for worrying about it, do you, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people you believe are watching you or sending you messages are actually doing so in a way that's real and not just in your mind?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... that's the hard part, isn't it? I would love nothing more than to just agree with you and say it's all in my head. I really don't want to be one of those difficult people who imagines silly things and makes a fuss over nothing. \n\nBut... well, like I was just saying a moment ago, that's really the whole reason I finally came in to see you. Because even on my very best days, when I'm feeling perfectly fine, I just can't shake the feeling that it *is* real. It truly feels like those folks at church and the ladies from the walking club are actually watching me in real life. And the things they say, or those little notes in the bulletin... it feels like they are genuinely putting them there just for me. \n\nI try so hard to tell myself it's just my imagination playing tricks on an old lady, but it just feels so very real to me. I hope you don't think less of me for admitting that. Am I making any sense at all, doctor? I just want to be as honest with you as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the messages you believe you're seeing and hearing are not actually intended for you?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose it's possible. I know it sounds so presumptuous of me to think everyone is focused on me. I really don't want to be one of those people who makes everything about themselves. \n\nBut... oh, it's just so hard to explain without sounding completely foolish. Like I mentioned, the whole reason I'm here taking up your time is because I just can't seem to make the feeling go away. Even when my mood is perfectly fine and I'm having a lovely day, I still get that deep-down feeling that the folks at church and the ladies in the walking club are watching me. And those little things in the bulletin, or the specific words they choose when we talk... it just feels so strongly like they're putting them there specifically for me to notice. \n\nI try my best to tell myself it's just a silly misunderstanding, but the feeling just won't leave me be. Does that make me sound terribly stubborn, doctor? I really am trying to look at it logically, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts or conversations often seem to jump abruptly from one topic to another without a clear connection?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well... I suppose I might do that just a little bit. I really hope I'm not being too confusing right now! I always want to be polite and make sure I'm making sense when I speak to people. \n\nBut sometimes... well, when I try to explain things, I might start out with one thought—maybe about the mail, or something that happened at church—and then, before I know it, I end up talking about quilt patterns, or the birds outside, or... or those little warnings I feel like I'm getting. And then I look at the person I'm talking to, and I can just see on their face that they don't have the faintest idea what I'm getting at. \n\nI feel so terrible when that happens, because I really don't mean to be difficult to follow. I just have so many thoughts and they sort of tumble out all at once. I hope I'm not doing that to you right now, doctor. Am I being clear enough for you?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to express your thoughts in a logical and coherent manner?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose it's a bit like what I was just saying. I really do try my best to be clear, I promise I do. I don't want to be a bother or make things hard for anyone to understand. \n\nBut sometimes, yes, I... I start out trying to explain something simple, like the mail or church, and then... well, my mind just wanders a bit. Before I know it, I'm talking about quilt patterns, or the birds at the feeder, or... or those little warnings I was telling you about. And I can just see in the other person's eyes that they're completely lost. \n\nIt makes me feel so embarrassed, doctor. I really want to be logical and make sense. I hope I'm doing an okay job explaining it to you now. Am I making sense, or am I rambling again?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to organize your thoughts when speaking or writing?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... I feel like I'm just repeating myself to you, and I really don't want to be a nuisance or waste your time! But yes, organizing them... it's exactly like I was trying to say a moment ago. \n\nWhether I'm speaking or even trying to write a little note, I'll start off with a perfectly normal thought about the mail or something at church. But then my mind just sort of... flutters, I suppose. And suddenly I'm going on about my quilt patterns, or the birds, or those warnings. I look up, and I can just tell the person has no idea what I'm getting at. \n\nI try so hard to keep it all neat and tidy in my head, I really do. Does that answer what you were asking, doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your ability to perform daily tasks or maintain relationships with others over the past eleven months?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, I wouldn't say there have been *significant* changes, really. I mean, I still live at home just fine, and I try to keep things as tidy as I can. I hope I'm not giving the impression that I can't take care of myself! \n\nBut... I suppose I have slowed down just a little bit. I haven't been going to my quilting gatherings lately, or the walking club. And my gardening... well, I've let that go just a tiny bit, too. It's just that I haven't felt quite up to being around people as much, and I didn't want to bring anyone down. \n\nMy daughter has been so sweet, though. She's started checking in on me more often, making sure I'm eating my meals and keeping track of my appointments. I feel a little bad that she has to do that, but she insists it's no trouble. I just try to be a good patient for her and for you. Does that make sense, doctor? I hope I'm answering your question properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to take care of yourself or manage your daily life over the past eleven months?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... I feel like I'm repeating myself again, and I really don't mean to be tedious! I know you're very busy. \n\nBut, well, it's pretty much just like I was saying. I'm still living at home, and I really do my best to keep things nice. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around in a mess! But... I suppose I have stepped back from things a little bit. I haven't been to the quilting gatherings or the walking club in a while, and the garden is... well, it's a bit overgrown right now. \n\nAnd my daughter, bless her heart, she has been coming by more often to make sure I'm eating properly and getting to my appointments, like this one. I do feel dreadful taking up her time, but she says she doesn't mind. I'm trying very hard to stay on top of things, I promise. Is that the sort of thing you were asking about, doctor? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble sticking to one topic when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Oh, my goodness... I'm so sorry, doctor, I feel like I'm just making you listen to the exact same things over and over again! I really don't want to be a bother. \n\nBut yes, it's... well, it's just like I mentioned a few minutes ago. I'll be talking to someone, and I'll try to start off making perfect sense—maybe mentioning something simple, like a letter I got in the mail, or something the pastor said at church. But then... I don't know, my mind just sort of drifts, I guess. Before I even realize it, I'm going on and on about my quilt patterns, or the birds outside my window, or... or those warnings I get. \n\nAnd then I'll look at the person's face, and I can just tell they are completely lost. They have no idea how I got from the church bulletin to my quilting squares. I try so hard to stay on track, I really do, because I don't want people to think I'm losing my wits! I hope I'm not making myself sound too scattered, doctor. Does that answer your question alright?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I hope I haven't made it sound worse than it actually is! I just get a bit scatterbrained and lose my place, but my mind certainly isn't speeding. \n\nDoes that help clarify things a bit, doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information and not making a mountain out of a molehill.\n\nDoctor: Have these experiences of being watched, hearing your name, or having trouble keeping your thoughts straight caused you any problems at work, at home, or in your relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say they've caused *problems*, exactly. I hope that doesn't make me sound like a burden to her, does it? I know how terribly silly it must sound when you say it out loud like that. I really don't want you to think I'm being vain, assuming everything is about me. \n\nBut... yes, I suppose I do believe they're for me. Honestly, that's the main reason I finally agreed to come in and talk to you today. Because even on my better days, when I'm feeling quite alright and keeping busy, I just can't seem to shake the feeling. \n\nIt just seems like some of the folks at church, and even a few of the ladies from the walking club, are... well, watching me. And those little things printed in the Sunday bulletin, or the very specific words they use when we're chatting... it truly feels like they are little messages put there just for me to notice. \n\nI try my hardest to just brush it off and keep a smile on my face, because I'd absolutely hate to cause a fuss or accuse anyone of doing anything improper. I hope that's the right way to handle it. You don't think I sound completely foolish for worrying about it, do you, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people you believe are watching you or sending you messages are actually doing so in a way that's real and not just in your mind?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... that's the hard part, isn't it? I would love nothing more than to just agree with you and say it's all in my head. I really don't want to be one of those difficult people who imagines silly things and makes a fuss over nothing. \n\nBut... well, like I was just saying a moment ago, that's really the whole reason I finally came in to see you. Because even on my very best days, when I'm feeling perfectly fine, I just can't shake the feeling that it *is* real. It truly feels like those folks at church and the ladies from the walking club are actually watching me in real life. And the things they say, or those little notes in the bulletin... it feels like they are genuinely putting them there just for me. \n\nI try so hard to tell myself it's just my imagination playing tricks on an old lady, but it just feels so very real to me. I hope you don't think less of me for admitting that. Am I making any sense at all, doctor? I just want to be as honest with you as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the messages you believe you're seeing and hearing are not actually intended for you?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose it's possible. I know it sounds so presumptuous of me to think everyone is focused on me. I really don't want to be one of those people who makes everything about themselves. \n\nBut... oh, it's just so hard to explain without sounding completely foolish. Like I mentioned, the whole reason I'm here taking up your time is because I just can't seem to make the feeling go away. Even when my mood is perfectly fine and I'm having a lovely day, I still get that deep-down feeling that the folks at church and the ladies in the walking club are watching me. And those little things in the bulletin, or the specific words they choose when we talk... it just feels so strongly like they're putting them there specifically for me to notice. \n\nI try my best to tell myself it's just a silly misunderstanding, but the feeling just won't leave me be. Does that make me sound terribly stubborn, doctor? I really am trying to look at it logically, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts or conversations often seem to jump abruptly from one topic to another without a clear connection?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... well... I suppose I might do that just a little bit. I really hope I'm not being too confusing right now! I always want to be polite and make sure I'm making sense when I speak to people. \n\nBut sometimes... well, when I try to explain things, I might start out with one thought—maybe about the mail, or something that happened at church—and then, before I know it, I end up talking about quilt patterns, or the birds outside, or... or those little warnings I feel like I'm getting. And then I look at the person I'm talking to, and I can just see on their face that they don't have the faintest idea what I'm getting at. \n\nI feel so terrible when that happens, because I really don't mean to be difficult to follow. I just have so many thoughts and they sort of tumble out all at once. I hope I'm not doing that to you right now, doctor. Am I being clear enough for you?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to express your thoughts in a logical and coherent manner?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose it's a bit like what I was just saying. I really do try my best to be clear, I promise I do. I don't want to be a bother or make things hard for anyone to understand. \n\nBut sometimes, yes, I... I start out trying to explain something simple, like the mail or church, and then... well, my mind just wanders a bit. Before I know it, I'm talking about quilt patterns, or the birds at the feeder, or... or those little warnings I was telling you about. And I can just see in the other person's eyes that they're completely lost. \n\nIt makes me feel so embarrassed, doctor. I really want to be logical and make sense. I hope I'm doing an okay job explaining it to you now. Am I making sense, or am I rambling again?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to organize your thoughts when speaking or writing?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... But then my mind just sort of... flutters, I suppose. And suddenly I'm going on about my quilt patterns, or the birds, or those warnings. I look up, and I can just tell the person has no idea what I'm getting at. \n\nI try so hard to keep it all neat and tidy in my head, I really do. Does that answer what you were asking, doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your ability to perform daily tasks or maintain relationships with others over the past eleven months?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, I wouldn't say there have been *significant* changes, really. She's started checking in on me more often, making sure I'm eating my meals and keeping track of my appointments. I feel a little bad that she has to do that, but she insists it's no trouble. I just try to be a good patient for her and for you. Does that make sense, doctor? Oh, doctor, I... well, I don't want to make a mountain out of a molehill, really. I know you're so busy and I don't want to complain too much.
